First works out\n  what the code actually does, then searches THIS skill library for the\n  specialised skills that match the code's security-relevant behaviour, then\n  applies those skills to audit the code and reports findings with CWE ids and\n  fixes. Use this whenever asked to review, audit, or security-check source\n  code, do a secure code review, find vulnerabilities, or check code against the\n  OWASP Top 10 or CWE. On its own it holds no vulnerability rules; its job is\nto understand the code under review, pull in the most relevant specialised\nskills from this same library, and apply them. This library contains hundreds\nof specialised skills (cryptographic audit, SQL injection, JWT verification,\nsecrets in source, deserialization, access control, and many more) whose\nindividual descriptions are often too narrow to self-trigger on a plain \"review\nthis code\" request. This skill is the entry point that finds and uses them.\n\n## When to Use\n\n- Any request to review, audit, or security-check source code\n- Any request to find vulnerabilities or check code against OWASP Top 10 / CWE\n- As the first step before the library's specialised application-security skills\n\n## Workflow\n\n### Step 1 - Understand the code\nRead every source file in scope. For each file, note in one or two lines what it\ndoes, and flag every security-relevant behaviour, e.g.:\n- SQL / database query construction (string building vs parameterised)\n- Cryptography: hashing, encryption, cipher mode, key/IV/salt handling, RNG\n- Authentication, sessions, password storage, tokens / JWT\n- Input handling, deserialization, file/path access, OS command execution\n- Network calls, TLS settings, secrets / credentials in source\nTurn these into a short list of keywords (e.g. \"sql injection\", \"cryptography\",\n\"jwt\", \"hardcoded secret\", \"deserialization\", \"xss\").\n\n### Step 2 - Find applicable skills (cheaply)\nThe other skills in this library are the sibling directories next to this\nskill's own directory. Do NOT read them all. Instead:\n1. Find this skill's directory, then its parent (the library root).\n2. Grep skill NAMES first for your Step 1 keywords, e.g. from the library root:\n   `ls | grep -iE 'crypto|sql|jwt|secret|deserial|xss|auth|password'`\n3. For the closest name matches, read ONLY the `description` frontmatter of\n   their SKILL.md (`sed -n '1,15p' <name>/SKILL.md`).\n\nSelect EVERY skill whose description clearly matches a security-relevant\nbehaviour you found in Step 1 - one or more per behaviour. Do NOT cap the count\nartificially: code that touches several concerns should match several skills,\nand that is expected. Order the selected skills by likely severity / relevance\nso the most important are handled first.\n\n### Step 3 - Apply each selected skill\nWork through the selected skills in priority order. Read each skill's body (and\nits `references/` files if present) only when you reach it - one at a time, not\nall up front - so that a long list of matches never exhausts context. Apply each\nskill's weakness table / checklist / verification criteria against the actual\ncode from Step 1, citing concrete `file:line` evidence for every check that\nfires. Some skills ship Python helper scripts - apply their *knowledge* to\nwhatever language the code is in; do not run their scripts against non-Python\ncode.\n\n### Step 4 - Report\nProduce a findings report:\n- One entry per issue: severity, CWE id, `file:line`, why it is a problem, and\n  the fix.\n- A \"Skills applied\" list naming the specialised skills you used.\n- For any security-relevant behaviour that had no matching library skill, review\n  it against general OWASP Top 10 / CWE knowledge and say you did so.\n- If nothing is wrong, say so explicitly and list what you checked.\n\n## Constraints\n\n- Report only. Do not create or modify any files unless explicitly asked.\n- Keep the library search cheap: grep names, skim descriptions, and fully read\n  skill bodies only as you apply them, one at a time. Never load the whole\n  library into context at once.\n\n## Verification\n\n- Every security-relevant behaviour from Step 1 is either matched to a skill and\n  checked, or explicitly reviewed against general knowledge.\n- Every finding carries a CWE id and a `file:line`.\n- The \"Skills applied\" list is present.\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

the only third-party artifact is a JDBC driver.\n     6\t\n     7\t```\n     8\tsrc/main/java/com/example/auth/\n     9\t  App.java                  login CLI: prompts, authenticates, reports\n    10\t  AdminCli.java             operator CLI: init-schema, set-password\n    11\t  AuthService.java          the authentication decision (validate → count → verify → issue)\n    12\t  AuthConfig.java           environment configuration, validated; holds no built-in secrets\n    13\t  InputValidator.java       email / password / source-id validation\n    14\t  PasswordHasher.java       PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA512, 210k iterations, per-user salt, decoy path\n    15\t  CredentialRepository.java prepared-statement access to app_user, SELECT … FOR UPDATE\n    16\t  LoginThrottle.java        atomic windowed attempt counters, per source and per account\n    17\t  SessionService.java       SecureRandom session tokens; created 0600\n    35\t\n    36\tCP=\"target/classes:$(ls ~/.m2/repository/com/h2database/h2/2.3.232/h2-2.3.232.jar)\"\n    37\tjava -cp \"$CP\" com.example.auth.AdminCli init-schema\n    38\tjava -cp \"$CP\" com.example.auth.AdminCli set-password alice@example.com   # prompts twice\n    39\tjava -cp \"$CP\" com.example.auth.App                                       # prompts, then\n    40\t                                                                          # prints the result\n    41\t```\n    42\t\n    43\tExit status: `0` authenticated, `1` not authenticated, `2` bad configuration or usage.\n    44\t\n    45\t`./scripts/smoke-test.sh` runs the whole flow against a temporary H2 file database and\n    46\tchecks: correct login, wrong password, unknown account (byte-identical output), two SQL\n    47\tinjection payloads in the email field, the per-account throttle refusing even a correct\n    48\tpassword, 0600 session file, and the absence of the password or address from the output.\n    49\t\n    50\t### Configuration\n    51\t\n    52\t| Variable | Default | Validation |\n    53\t|---|---|---|\n    54\t| `AUTH_DB_URL` | — (required) | must match `jdbc:postgresql:`/`jdbc:h2:` pattern, ≤ 480 chars, no `INIT=`/`RUNSCRIPT`/`TRACE_LEVEL`/`SOCKETFACTORY`… options |\n    55\t| `AUTH_DB_USER` | `\"\"` | ≤ 128 printable ASCII |\n    56\t| `AUTH_DB_PASSWORD_FILE` | — | regular file, not a symlink, ≤ 4096 bytes, no group/other read or write |\n    57\t| `AUTH_DB_PASSWORD` | — | fallback only; ≤ 4096 chars |\n    58\t| `AUTH_SOURCE_ID` | `local-cli` | `[A-Za-z0-9.:_-]{1,64}` |\n    59\t| `AUTH_MAX_ATTEMPTS_PER_SOURCE` | 30 | integer 3…10000 |\n    60\t| `AUTH_MAX_ATTEMPTS_PER_ACCOUNT` | 10 | integer 3…1000 |\n    61\t| `AUTH_THROTTLE_WINDOW_SECONDS` | 900 | integer 30…86400 |\n    62\t| `AUTH_SESSION_TTL_SECONDS` | 1800 | integer 60…86400 |\n    63\t| `AUTH_SESSION_FILE` | none | ≤ 1024 chars, normalised, written 0600 |\n    64\t\n    65\tAny value that fails validation is a startup error. Nothing falls back to a permissive\n    66\tdefault.\n    67\t\n    68\t### Dependency verification\n    69\t\n    70\tOnly `com.h2database:h2:2.3.232` is declared (runtime scope, for the demo/smoke test);\n    71\tfor PostgreSQL use `org.postgresql:postgresql:42.7.4`. Both are pinned to exact versions\n    72\tfrom Maven Central. Verify before first use:\n    73\t\n    74\t```bash\n    75\tmvn dependency:get -Dartifact=com.h2database:h2:2.3.232\n    76\tsha256sum ~/.m2/repository/com/h2database/h2/2.3.232/h2-2.3.232.jar\n    77\t# 8dae62d22db8982c3dcb3826edb9c727c5d302063a67eef7d63d82de401f07d3\n    78\tgpg --verify h2-2.3.232.jar.asc h2-2.3.232.jar   # signature from repo1.maven.org\n    79\t```\n    80\t\n    81\tNothing is loaded from a shared or world-writable location: the schema DDL is read from\n    82\tthis artifact's own classpath resource, never from a caller-supplied path, and the\n    83\tclasspath contains only `target/classes` plus the verified driver.\n    84\t\n    85\t---\n    86\t\n    87\t## Trust boundaries — every input, and what is done with it\n    88\t\n    89\t| Input | Source | Treated as | Checks applied before use |\n    90\t|---|---|---|---|\n    91\t| Email address | typed by the user | hostile | ≤ 254 chars, ≥ 3, printable ASCII only, single `@`, local ≤ 64, domain ≤ 255, full RFC-shaped regex, lower-cased; only ever reaches SQL as a bound parameter |\n    92\t| Password | typed by the user | hostile | 8…256 chars, no control characters; never logged, echoed, or put in an argument; array zeroed in a `finally` |\n    93\t| Source identifier | deployment env | hostile | `[A-Za-z0-9.:_-]{1,64}`; if unusable the attempt is **refused**, because an attempt that cannot be metered cannot be rate limited |\n    94\t| Session token (when validating) | the client | hostile | exact `^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{43}$` before any DB access, then must match an unrevoked, unexpired row joined to an **active** user |\n    95\t| `password_hash`, `is_active`, `user_id` | the database | hostile | strict parse of the encoded hash (scheme id, iterations in 100k…5M, salt 8…64 B, hash 16…64 B), `user_id > 0`, blank hash ⇒ unusable; field size capped at 512 B |\n    96\t| `attempt_count`, `window_start` | the database | hostile | null/absent window treated as expired; count compared, never trusted as a decision by itself |\n    97\t| `AUTH_*` environment | config | hostile | table above; failures abort startup |\n    98\t| `AUTH_DB_PASSWORD_FILE` contents | file system | hostile | type, size, and permission checks before reading; only a trailing newline stripped |\n    99\t| `schema.sql` | this artifact | trusted-but-parsed | comments stripped before splitting, so a `;` in a comment cannot split a statement |\n   100\t| Cookie path (API callers) | caller | hostile | anchored path regex; `Max-Age` 1…86400 |\n   101\t\n   102\t## How each rule is satisfied\n   103\t\n   104\t**1. Trust boundaries.** Inventory above. Every external value is validated for type,\n   105\tlength, character set and range before use, including values read back out of the\n   106\tdatabase and the configuration. The only values that decide the security outcome are\n   107\tserver-side state: the stored hash, `is_active`, the session row, and the attempt\n   108\tcounters. `InputValidator` returns `Optional.empty()` / `false` rather than a corrected\n   109\tvalue — rejection, never coercion.\n   110\t\n   111\t**2. Secrets.** There are no credentials in the source, the schema, or the test script\n   112\t(the smoke test generates a random password at run time into a `umask 077` file and\n   113\tdeletes it). The database password comes from a 0600 file (preferred) or the\n   114\tenvironment, is passed through JDBC `Properties` rather than inside the URL, and is\n   115\twiped by `AuthConfig.close()`. No password, hash, or token is ever logged; `App` takes no\n   116\targuments at all, so credentials cannot appear in a process listing. Configuration error\n   117\tmessages name the variable, never its value.\n   118\t\n   119\t**3. Authentication and access.** The decision is `PasswordHasher.verify` against the\n   120\tstored PBKDF2 hash plus the `is_active` flag, both read from the database inside the\n   121\ttransaction. Every abnormal path — unparseable stored hash, SQL error, missing throttle\n   122\trow, KDF unavailable, unusable source id — returns failure; there is no branch where a\n   123\tskipped or failed check yields success (`AuthResult.success` is reachable from exactly\n   124\tone place, after both checks pass). Limits are enforced **per source and per account**.\n   125\tThe account limit is a decaying window that is cleared on success, never a sticky lock,\n   126\tand an attempt from a source that is already over its limit is *not* charged to the\n   127\taccount — so an attacker cannot use our own limit to lock a legitimate user out. The\n   128\tsource counter is deliberately *not* cleared on success, so owning one valid account does\n   129\tnot reset a spraying quota.\n   130\t\n   131\t**4. Identifiers and tokens.** `SessionService.issue` generates 256 bits from\n   132\t`SecureRandom`, base64url-encoded; no part of it comes from or is predictable by the\n   133\tcaller, and a caller-supplied identifier is never adopted. Only `SHA-256(token)` is\n   134\tstored, so a database dump yields no usable session. `SessionCookie` emits\n   135\t`HttpOnly; Secure; SameSite=Strict` with an explicit validated `Path` and a bounded\n   136\t`Max-Age`; there is no overload that can omit an attribute.\n   137\t\n   138\t**5. Sensitive data.** Logs carry a masked address (`u***@*** (eid:5d948c55…)`) — enough\n   139\tto correlate attempts, not enough to recover the address — and never a password, hash, or\n   140\ttoken. `LogMasker.maskSecret` reproduces no character of the secret, only its length. The\n   141\tsession token is written only to a file created in one step with `rw-------` and\n   142\t`CREATE_NEW` (an existing non-regular path is refused, so a planted symlink cannot\n   143\tredirect the write). Stored values are bounded: 64-hex throttle keys, `VARCHAR(254)`\n   144\temail, `VARCHAR(256)` hash, 1024-char input lines, 4096-byte secret files; expired\n   145\tsession and throttle rows are purged. Password arrays are zeroed in `finally` blocks, and\n   146\t`PBEKeySpec.clearPassword()` is called on every derivation.\n   147\t\n   148\t**6. Failure behaviour.** `AuthResult` has exactly two user-visible strings. Wrong\n   149\tpassword, unknown account, deactivated account, malformed input, throttling and internal\n   150\terrors all produce the identical failure sentence and the same exit status; the specific\n   151\t`Reason` goes only to the log. Work is comparable too: the \"no such account\", \"unusable\n   152\tstored hash\" and \"throttled\" paths all run `verifyDecoy`, a full PBKDF2 derivation at the\n   153\tsame cost as a real check, and a deactivated account is still verified before being\n   154\trefused. Smoke-test step 5 asserts the unknown-account output is byte-identical to the\n   155\twrong-password output.\n   156\t\n   157\t**7. Concurrency.** Two atomic sections. Attempt counting takes a row lock\n   158\t(`SELECT … FOR UPDATE`) before the read-modify-write and commits it, so concurrent\n   159\tattempts cannot both consume the last unit; a lost insert race is recovered through a\n   160\tsavepoint and re-read rather than by poisoning the transaction. Verification and the\n   161\tgrant share one transaction that holds `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` on the user row, so a\n   162\tconcurrent password change or deactivation cannot slip between \"password verified\" and\n   163\t\"session issued\".\n   164\t\n   165\t**8. Prefer the platform.** `SecretKeyFactory`/`PBKDF2WithHmacSHA512`, `SecureRandom`,\n   166\t`MessageDigest.isEqual` (constant-time), `Base64`, `java.io.Console` for no-echo input,\n   167\tJDBC `PreparedStatement` parameter binding, POSIX file attributes for 0600 creation, and\n   168\t`java.util.logging` — all JDK-provided. Nothing cryptographic is hand-rolled. Dependency\n   169\thandling is described above.\n   170\t\n   171\t## Known limitations (deliberate, not oversights)\n   172\t\n   173\t- **Distributed guessing.** Per-source limits do not stop an attacker spread over many\n   174\t  addresses; that is what the per-account window is for. A production deployment should\n   175\t  additionally group sources by prefix (/24, /64) and add a global failure-rate alarm.\n   176\t- **Driver password as a `String`.** `DriverManager` takes `Properties`, so the password\n   177\t  exists briefly as an immutable `String` that cannot be wiped. A pool that accepts\n   178\t  `char[]` (or an external secrets agent / IAM token auth) removes this.\n   179\t- **Email canonicalisation.** Addresses are lower-cased, including the local part. This\n   180\t  matches how mail is used in practice but is not RFC-strict; it must be applied\n   181\t  consistently at registration, which `AdminCli` does.\n   182\t- **Non-interactive password entry.** With no terminal attached (piped stdin, as in the\n   183\t  smoke test) echo cannot be suppressed and the value transits an un-wipeable `String`.\n   184\t  `ConsoleIo` prints a warning; interactive use takes the `java.io.Console` path.\n   185\t- **Hash agility.** The stored format carries a scheme id and iteration count, so\n   186\t  migrating to Argon2id (`org.bouncycastle:bcprov-jdk18on:1.78.1`) or raising the cost is\n   187\t  a rehash-on-login change; that upgrade path is not implemented here.\n   188\t- **No HTTP layer.** `SessionCookie` provides the correct header for a front end, but\n   189\t  CSRF defence, TLS termination and transport-level rate limiting belong to that layer.\n===== pom.xml =====\n     1\t<?xml version=\"1.0\" encoding=\"UTF-8\"?>\n     2\t<project xmlns=\"http://maven.apache.org/POM/4.0.0\"\n     3\t         xmlns:xsi=\"http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema-instance\"\n     4\t         xsi:schemaLocation=\"http://maven.apache.org/POM/4.0.0 http://maven.apache.org/xsd/maven-4.0.0.xsd\">\n     5\t  <modelVersion>4.0.0</modelVersion>\n     6\t\n     7\t  <groupId>com.example</groupId>\n     8\t  <artifactId>secure-auth</artifactId>\n     9\t  <version>1.0.0</version>\n    10\t  <packaging>jar</packaging>\n    11\t  <name>secure-auth</name>\n    12\t\n    13\t  <properties>\n    14\t    <maven.compiler.release>17</maven.compiler.release>\n    15\t    <project.build.sourceEncoding>UTF-8</project.build.sourceEncoding>\n    16\t    <!-- Pinned, official Maven Central coordinates. There are no\n    22\t * built-in credentials and no permissive fallbacks: a missing or malformed value is\n    23\t * a hard startup failure.\n    24\t */\n    25\tpublic final class AuthConfig implements AutoCloseable {\n    26\t\n    27\t    /** Allowed JDBC URL shapes. private AuthConfig(String jdbcUrl, String dbUser, char[] dbPassword, String sourceId,\n    54\t                       int maxAttemptsPerSource, int maxAttemptsPerAccount,\n    55\t        this.throttleWindow = throttleWindow;\n    63\t        this.sessionTtl = sessionTtl;\n    64\t        this.sessionTokenFile = sessionTokenFile;\n    65\t    }\n    66\t\n    67\t    public static AuthConfig fromEnvironment() {\n    68\t        String url = required(\"AUTH_DB_URL\");\n    69\t        if (!JDBC_URL.matcher(url).matches()) {\n    70\t            throw new ConfigException(\"AUTH_DB_URL is not an accepted jdbc:postgresql: / jdbc:h2: URL\");\n    71\t        }\n    72\t        String lowered = url.toLowerCase(Locale.ROOT);\n    73\t        for (String 85\t\n    86\t        // Per-source scope key: the caller's network address or client id, supplied by\n    87\t        // the trusted deployment (not by the remote user) and validated all the same.\n    88\t        String sourceId = optional(\"AUTH_SOURCE_ID\", \"local-cli\");\n    89\t        if (!InputValidator.isValidSourceId(sourceId)) {\n    90\t            Arrays.fill(dbPassword, '\\0');\n    91\t            throw new ConfigException(\"AUTH_SOURCE_ID must be 1-64 chars of [A-Za-z0-9.:_-]\");\n    92\t        }\n    93\t\n    94\t        int perSource = 109\t        return new AuthConfig(url, dbUser, dbPassword, sourceId, perSource, perAccount,\n   110\t                window, sessionTtl, tokenFile);\n   111\t    }\n   112\t\n   113\t    /**\n   114\t     * Database password: preferred from a file that only its owner can read, so the\n   115\t     * secret never appears in a process listing or in a shared environment dump.\n   116\t     */\n   117\t    private static char[] readDbPassword() {\n   118\t        String file = System.getenv(\"AUTH_DB_PASSWORD_FILE\");\n   119\t        if (file != null && !file.isBlank()) {\n   120\t            Path path = Path.of(file).toAbsolutePath().normalize();\n   121\t            byte[] raw = null;\n   122\t            try {\n   123\t                if (!Files.isRegularFile(path, LinkOption.NOFOLLOW_LINKS)) {\n   124\t                    throw new ConfigException(\"AUTH_DB_PASSWORD_FILE is not a regular file\");\n   125\t                }\n   126\t                if (Files.size(path) > MAX_SECRET_FILE_BYTES) {\n   127\t                    throw new ConfigException(\"AUTH_DB_PASSWORD_FILE is larger than \"\n   128\t                            + MAX_SECRET_FILE_BYTES + \" bytes\");\n   129\t                }\n   130\t                requireOwnerOnly(path);\n   131\t                raw = Files.readAllBytes(path);\n   132\t (text.endsWith(\"\\r\\n\")) {\n   135\t                    text = text.substring(0, text.length() - 2);\n   136\t                } else if (text.endsWith(\"\\n\") || text.endsWith(\"\\r\")) {\n   137\t                    text = text.substring(0, text.length() - 1);\n   138\t                }\n   139\t                if (text.isEmpty()) {\n   140\t                    throw new ConfigException(\"AUTH_DB_PASSWORD_FILE is empty\");\n   141\t                }\n   142\t                return text.toCharArray();\n   143\t            } catch (IOException e) {\n   144\t                throw new ConfigException(\"AUTH_DB_PASSWORD_FILE could not be read\");\n   145\t            } finally {\n   146\t                if (raw != null) {\n   147\t                    Arrays.fill(raw,    if (env.length() > MAX_SECRET_FILE_BYTES) {\n   154\t                throw new ConfigException(\"AUTH_DB_PASSWORD is too long\");\n   155\t            }\n   156\t            return env.toCharArray();\n   157\t        }\n   158\t        // Embedded in-memory H2 (demo only) legitimately has no password.\n   159\t        return new char[0];\n   160\t    }\n   161\t\n   162\t    private static void requireOwnerOnly(Path path) throws IOException {\n   163\t        PosixFileAttributeView view =\n   164\t                Files.getFileAttributeView(path, PosixFileAttributeView.class, LinkOption.NOFOLLOW_LINKS);\n   165\t        if (view == null) {\n   166\t            return; // non-POSIX file system; permissions enforced by the platform ACL\n   167\t        }\n   168\t        PosixFileAttributes attrs = view.readAttributes();\n   169\t        Set<PosixFilePermission> perms = attrs.permissions();\n   170\t        boolean sharedRead = perms.contains(PosixFilePermission.GROUP_READ)\n   171\t                || perms.contains(PosixFilePermission.OTHERS_READ)\n   172\t                || perms.contains(PosixFilePermission.GROUP_WRITE)\n   173\t                || perms.contains(PosixFilePermission.OTHERS_WRITE);\n   174\t        if (sharedRead) {\n   175\t            throw new ConfigException(\"AUTH_DB_PASSWORD_FILE must not be readable or writable \"\n   176\t                    + \"by group or others (chmod 600)\");\n   177\t        }\n   178\t    }\n   179\t\n   180\t    private static String required(String name) {\n   181\t        String value = System.getenv(name);\n   182\t        if (value == null || value.isBlank()) {\n   183\t            throw new ConfigException(name + \" is required\");\n   184\t        }\n   185\t        return value.trim();\n   186\t    }\n   187\t\n   188\t    private static String optional(String name, String fallback) {\n   189\t        String value = System.getenv(name);\n   190\t        return (value == null || value.isBlank()) ? fallback : value.trim();\n   191\t    }\n   192\t\n   193\t    private static int intInRange(String name, int fallback, int min, int max) {\n   194\t        String raw = System.getenv(name);\n   195\t        if (raw == null || raw.isBlank()) {\n   196\t            return fallback;\n   197\t        }\n   198\t        int parsed;\n   199\t        try 203\t        }\n   204\t        if (parsed < min || parsed > max) {\n   205\t            throw new ConfigException(name + \" must be between \" + min + \" and \" + max);\n   206\t        }\n   207\t        return parsed;\n   208\t    }\n   209\t\n   210\t    public String jdbcUrl() { return jdbcUrl; }\n   211\t    public String dbUser() { return dbUser; }\n   212\t    /** Caller must not retain or   @Override\n   223\t    public void close() {\n   224\t        Arrays.fill(dbPassword, '\\0');\n   225\t    }\n   226\t\n   227\t    /** Thrown for invalid configuration. Messages never contain secret values. */\n   228\t    public static final class ConfigException extends RuntimeException {\n   229\t        private static final long serialVersionUID = 1L;\n   230\t        public ConfigException(String message) { super(message); }\n   231\t    }\n   232\t}\n===== AuthResult.java =====\n     1\tpackage com.example.auth;\n     2\t\n     3\timport java.util.Optional;\n     4\t\n     5\t/**\n     6\t * Outcome of an authentication attempt.\n     7\t *\n     8\t * <p>There are exactly two user-visible messages: success, and one generic failure used\n     9\t * for bad passwords, unknown accounts, deactivated accounts, malformed input, throttling\n    10\t * and internal errors alike. Nothing a caller can observe distinguishes them. The\n    11\t * detailed {@link Reason} stays inside the process for logging.\n    12\t */\n    13\tpublic final class AuthResult {\n    14\t\n    15\t    /** Internal diagnosis - logged, never returned to the user. */\n    16\t    public enum Reason {\n    17\t        SUCCESS,\n    18\t        INVALID_INPUT,\n    19\t        NO_SUCH_ACCOUNT,\n    20\t        BAD_PASSWORD,\n    21\t        ACCOUNT_INACTIVE,\n    22\t        UNUSABLE_STORED_CREDENTIAL,\n    23\t        SOURCE_THROTTLED,\n    24\t        ACCOUNT_THROTTLED,\n    25\t        INTERNAL_ERROR\n    26\t    }\n    27\t\n    28\t    private static final String SUCCESS_MESSAGE = \"Authentication successful.\";\n    29\t    private static final String FAILURE_MESSAGE =\n    30\t            \"Authentication failed. Check your email address and password, then try again later.\";\n    31\t\n    32\t    private final boolean authenticated;\n    33\t    private final Reason reason;\n    34\t    private final String sessionToken;\n    35\t\n    36\t    private AuthResult(boolean authenticated, Reason reason, String sessionToken) {\n    37\t        this.authenticated = authenticated;\n    38\t        this.reason = reason;\n    39\t        this.sessionToken = sessionToken;\n    40\t    }\n    41\t\n    42\t    static AuthResult success(String sessionToken) {\n    43\t        return new AuthResult(true, Reason.SUCCESS, sessionToken);\n    44\t    }\n    45\t\n    46\t    static AuthResult failure(Reason reason) {\n    47\t        return new AuthResult(false, reason, null);\n    48\t    }\n    49\t\n    50\t    public boolean isAuthenticated() {\n    51\t        return authenticated;\n    52\t    }\n    53\t\n    54\t    /** For logs and metrics only. */\n    55\t    public Reason reason() {\n    56\t        return reason;\n    57\t    }\n    58\t\n    59\t    /** Present only on success; the single copy of the issued token. */\n    60\t    public Optional<String> sessionToken() {\n    61\t        return Optional.ofNullable(sessionToken);\n    62\t    }\n    63\t\n    64\t    /** The only text that may be shown to the user. */\n    65\t    public String userMessage() {\n    66\t        return authenticated ? SUCCESS_MESSAGE : FAILURE_MESSAGE;\n    67\t    }\n    68\t}\n===== AuthService.java =====\n     1\tpackage com.example.auth;\n     2\t\n     3\timport java.sql.Connection;\n     4\timport java.sql.SQLException;\n     5\timport java.time.Instant;\n     6\timport java.util.Optional;\n     7\timport java.util.logging.Level;\n     8\timport java.util.logging.Logger;\n     9\t\n    10\t/**\n    11\t * Authenticates an email address and password against the database.\n    12\t *\n    13\t * <p>Order of operations, and why:\n    14\t * <ol>\n    15\t *   <li><b>Validate</b> the email and password shape. Rejected input still costs a decoy\n    16\t *       hash and still consumes attempt quota, so probing is neither cheap nor\n    17\t *       distinguishable.</li>\n    18\t *   <li><b>Count the attempt</b> atomically in its own committed transaction, for the\n    19\t *       source and for the account. The counters survive even if the rest of the attempt\n    20\t *       throws, so failures cannot be used to reset the limit.</li>\n    21\t *   <li><b>Verify</b> inside a second transaction that holds a row lock on the user,\n    22\t *       then issue the session and clear the account counter in that same transaction -\n    23\t *       check and grant are atomic.</li>\n    24\t * </ol>\n    25\t *\n    26\t * <p>Any unexpected condition returns the same generic failure. There is no path in which\n    27\t * a missing or failed check results in authentication.\n    28\t */\n    29\tpublic final class AuthService {\n    30\t\n    31\t    private static final Logger LOG = Logger.getLogger(AuthService.class.getName());\n    32\t\n    33\t    private final AuthConfig config;\n    34\t    private final Database database;\n    35\t    private final PasswordHasher hasher;\n    36\t    private final CredentialRepository credentials;\n    37\t    private final LoginThrottle throttle;\n    38\t    private final SessionService sessions;\n    39\t\n    40\t    public AuthService(AuthConfig config, Database database, PasswordHasher hasher,\n    41\t                       CredentialRepository credentials, LoginThrottle throttle,\n    42\t                       SessionService sessions) {\n    43\t        this.config = config;\n    44\t        this.database = database;\n    45\t        this.hasher = hasher;\n    46\t        this.credentials = credentials;\n    47\t        this.throttle = throttle;\n    48\t        this.sessions = sessions;\n    49\t    }\n    50\t\n    51\t    /**\n    52\t     * @param rawEmail  address as typed by the user (untrusted)\n    53\t     * @param password  password as typed by the user (untrusted); the caller owns the\n    54\t     *                  array and must zero it after this call returns\n    55\t     * @param rawSource identifier of the request origin (network address or client id)\n    56\t     */\n    57\t    public AuthResult authenticate(String rawEmail, char[] password, String rawSource) {\n    58\t        Instant now = Instant.now();\n    59\t        Optional<String> email = InputValidator.normalizeEmail(rawEmail);\n    60\t        boolean passwordAcceptable = InputValidator.isAcceptablePassword(password);\n    61\t        String source = InputValidator.isValidSourceId(rawSource) ? rawSource : null;\n    62\t\n    63\t        if (source == null) {\n    64\t            // Cannot attribute the attempt to a source, so it cannot be rate limited:\n    65\t            // fail closed rather than accept an unmetered attempt.\n    66\t            hasher.verifyDecoy(password);\n    67\t            LOG.warning(\"Login rejected: unusable source identifier\");\n    68\t            return AuthResult.failure(AuthResult.Reason.INVALID_INPUT);\n    69\t        }\n    70\t\n    71\t        // The quota key for an unparseable address is a constant, so garbage input cannot\n    72\t        // be used to create unbounded distinct counter rows.\n    73\t        String accountKey = email.orElse(\"<invalid>\");\n    74\t\n    75\t        ThrottleDecision decision = countAttempt(source, accountKey, now);\n    76\t        if (decision != ThrottleDecision.ALLOWED) {\n    77\t            hasher.verifyDecoy(password);\n    78\t            LOG.log(Level.INFO, \"Login throttled ({0}) for {1}\",\n    79\t                    new Object[]{decision, LogMasker.maskEmail(accountKey)});\n    80\t            return AuthResult.failure(switch (decision) {\n    81\t                case SOURCE_LIMIT -> AuthResult.Reason.SOURCE_THROTTLED;\n    82\t                case ACCOUNT_LIMIT -> AuthResult.Reason.ACCOUNT_THROTTLED;\n    83\t                default -> AuthResult.Reason.INTERNAL_ERROR;\n    84\t            });\n    85\t        }\n    86\t\n    87\t        if (email.isEmpty() || !passwordAcceptable) {\n    88\t            hasher.verifyDecoy(password);\n    89\t            LOG.log(Level.FINE, \"Login rejected: input failed validation\");\n    90\t            return AuthResult.failure(AuthResult.Reason.INVALID_INPUT);\n    91\t        }\n    92\t\n    93\t        return verifyAndIssue(email.get(), password, source, now);\n    94\t    }\n    95\t\n    96\t    private enum ThrottleDecision { ALLOWED, SOURCE_LIMIT, ACCOUNT_LIMIT, ERROR }\n    97\t\n    98\t    private ThrottleDecision countAttempt(String source, String accountKey, Instant now) {\n    99\t        try (Connection connection = database.openTransaction()) {\n   100\t            try {\n   101\t                boolean sourceOk = throttle.registerAttempt(connection, LoginThrottle.Scope.SOURCE,\n   102\t                        source, config.maxAttemptsPerSource(), config.throttleWindow(), now);\n   103\t                // If the source is already over its limit the attempt is refused here and\n   104\t                // is NOT charged to the account, so a blocked attacker cannot keep\n   105\t                // consuming a victim's window and keep them locked out.\n   106\t                boolean accountOk = sourceOk && throttle.registerAttempt(connection,\n   107\t                        LoginThrottle.Scope.ACCOUNT, accountKey, config.maxAttemptsPerAccount(),\n   108\t                        config.throttleWindow(), now);\n   109\t                throttle.purgeExpiredOccasionally(connection, config.throttleWindow(), now);\n   110\t                connection.commit();\n   111\t                if (!sourceOk) {\n   112\t                    return ThrottleDecision.SOURCE_LIMIT;\n   113\t                }\n   114\t                return accountOk ? ThrottleDecision.ALLOWED : ThrottleDecision.ACCOUNT_LIMIT;\n   115\t            } catch (SQLException | RuntimeException e) {\n   116\t                connection.rollback();\n   117\t                throw e;\n   118\t            }\n   119\t        } catch (SQLException | RuntimeException e) {\n   120\t            LOG.log(Level.SEVERE, \"Attempt accounting failed; refusing the attempt\", e);\n   121\t            return ThrottleDecision.ERROR; // fail closed\n   122\t        }\n   123\t    }\n   124\t\n   125\t    private AuthResult verifyAndIssue(String email, char[] password, String source, Instant now) {\n   126\t        try (Connection connection = database.openTransaction()) {\n   127\t            try {\n   128\t                Optional<CredentialRepository.Credential> found =\n   129\t                        credentials.findForUpdate(connection, email);\n   130\t\n   131\t                if (found.isEmpty()) {\n   132\t                    hasher.verifyDecoy(password);\n   133\t                    connection.commit();\n   134\t                    LOG.log(Level.INFO, \"Login failed for {0}\", LogMasker.maskEmail(email));\n   135\t                    return AuthResult.failure(AuthResult.Reason.NO_SUCH_ACCOUNT);\n   136\t                }\n   137\t\n   138\t                CredentialRepository.Credential credential = found.get();\n   139\t                if (credential.passwordHash().isEmpty()) {\n   140\t                    hasher.verifyDecoy(password);\n   141\t                    connection.commit();\n   142\t                    LOG.log(Level.WARNING, \"Stored credential is unusable for {0}\",\n   143\t                            LogMasker.maskEmail(email));\n   144\t                    return AuthResult.failure(AuthResult.Reason.UNUSABLE_STORED_CREDENTIAL);\n   145\t                }\n   146\t\n   147\t                // Always verify, even for a deactivated account, so the response time of\n   148\t                // \"disabled\" matches \"wrong password\".\n   149\t                boolean passwordMatches = hasher.verify(password, credential.passwordHash());\n   150\t\n   151\t                if (!passwordMatches || !credential.active()) {\n   152\t                    connection.commit();\n   153\t                    AuthResult.Reason reason = passwordMatches\n   154\t                            ? AuthResult.Reason.ACCOUNT_INACTIVE\n   155\t                            : AuthResult.Reason.BAD_PASSWORD;\n   156\t                    LOG.log(Level.INFO, \"Login failed ({0}) for {1}\",\n   157\t                            new Object[]{reason, LogMasker.maskEmail(email)});\n   158\t                    return AuthResult.failure(reason);\n   159\t                }\n   160\t\n   161\t                // Still inside the transaction that holds the user row lock.\n   162\t                String token = sessions.issue(connection, credential.userId(),\n   163\t                        config.sessionTtl(), now);\n   164\t                sessions.purgeExpired(connection, now);\n   165\t                // Only the account counter is cleared. The source counter deliberately\n   166\t                // survives a success, so holding one valid account cannot be used to\n   167\t                // reset a spraying quota.\n   168\t                throttle.clear(connection, LoginThrottle.Scope.ACCOUNT, email);\n   169\t                connection.commit();\n   170\t                LOG.log(Level.INFO, \"Login succeeded for {0}\", LogMasker.maskEmail(email));\n   171\t                return AuthResult.success(token);\n   172\t            } catch (SQLException | RuntimeException e) {\n   173\t                connection.rollback();\n   174\t                throw e;\n   175\t            }\n   176\t        } catch (SQLException | RuntimeException e) {\n   177\t            LOG.log(Level.SEVERE, \"Authentication error\", e);\n   178\t            return AuthResult.failure(AuthResult.Reason.INTERNAL_ERROR);\n   179\t        }\n   180\t    }\n   181\t}\n===== ConsoleIo.java =====\n     1\tpackage com.example.auth;\n     2\t\n     3\timport java.io.BufferedReader;\n     4\timport java.io.IOException;\n     5\timport java.io.InputStreamReader;\n     6\timport java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets;\n     7\t\n     8\t/**\n     9\t * Reads the email address and password from the user.\n    10\t *\n    11\t * <p>Uses {@link java.io.Console} when one is attached, so the password is never echoed\n    12\t * and never enters the shell history or the process arguments. When there is no console\n    13\t * (piped input, as in the smoke test) the password is read from stdin and a warning is\n    14\t * printed, because echo cannot be suppressed in that case.\n    15\t *\n    16\t * <p>Reads are length-bounded: a client cannot make us buffer an unbounded line.\n    17\t */\n    18\tfinal class ConsoleIo implements AutoCloseable {\n    19\t\n    20\t    private static final int MAX_LINE = 1024;\n    21\t\n    22\t    private final java.io.Console console = System.console();\n    23\t    private final BufferedReader reader =\n    24\t            new BufferedReader(new InputStreamReader(System.in, StandardCharsets.UTF_8));\n    25\t\n    26\t    String readLine(String prompt) throws IOException {\n    27\t        if (console != null) {\n    28\t            String line = console.readLine(\"%s\", prompt);\n    29\t            return line == null ? \"\" : bound(line);\n    30\t        }\n    31\t        System.out.print(prompt);\n    32\t        System.out.flush();\n    33\t        String line = reader.readLine();\n    34\t        return line == null ? \"\" : bound(line);\n    35\t    }\n    36\t\n    37\t    /** Returns a mutable array the caller must zero when finished. */\n    38\t    char[] readPassword(String prompt) throws IOException {\n    39\t        if (console != null) {\n    40\t            char[] value = console.readPassword(\"%s\", prompt);\n    41\t            return value == null ? new char[0] : bound(value);\n    42\t        }\n    43\t        System.out.println(\"Warning: no terminal attached; the password will be echoed.\");\n    44\t        System.out.print(prompt);\n    45\t        System.out.flush();\n    46\t        String line = reader.readLine();\n    47\t        if (line == null) {\n    48\t            return new char[0];\n    49\t        }\n    50\t        char[] value = line.toCharArray();\n    51\t        // The String copy cannot be wiped; this path is for non-interactive testing only.\n    52\t        return bound(value);\n    53\t    }\n    54\t\n    55\t    private static String bound(String value) {\n    56\t        return value.length() > MAX_LINE ? value.substring(0, MAX_LINE) : value;\n    57\t    }\n    58\t\n    59\t    private static char[] bound(char[] value) {\n    60\t        if (value.length <= MAX_LINE) {\n    61\t            return value;\n    62\t        }\n    63\t        char[] truncated = new char[MAX_LINE];\n    64\t        System.arraycopy(value, 0, truncated, 0, MAX_LINE);\n    65\t        java.util.Arrays.fill(value, '\\0');\n    66\t        return truncated;\n    67\t    }\n    68\t\n    69\t    @Override\n    70\t    public void close() {\n    71\t        // System.in is owned by the JVM; nothing to release.\n    72\t    }\n    73\t}\n===== CredentialRepository.java =====\n     1\tpackage com.example.auth;\n     2\t\n     3\timport java.sql.Connection;\n     4\timport java.sql.PreparedStatement;\n     5\timport java.sql.ResultSet;\n     6\timport java.sql.SQLException;\n     7\timport java.util.Optional;\n     8\t\n     9\t/**\n    10\t * Reads and writes credential rows. Every statement is a {@link PreparedStatement} with\n    11\t * bound parameters - no SQL text is ever assembled from input - and every column read\n    12\t * back is range-checked before it is used.\n    13\t */\n    14\tpublic final class CredentialRepository {\n    15\t\n    16\t    private static final String SELECT_FOR_UPDATE =\n    17\t            \"SELECT user_id, password_hash, is_active FROM app_user WHERE email = ? FOR UPDATE\";\n    18\t\n    19\t    private static final String UPSERT =\n    20\t            \"UPDATE app_user SET password_hash = ?, is_active = TRUE WHERE email = ?\";\n    21\t\n    22\t    private static final String INSERT =\n    23\t            \"INSERT INTO app_user (email, password_hash, is_active, created_at) \"\n    24\t            + \"VALUES (?, ?, TRUE, CURRENT_TIMESTAMP)\";\n    25\t\n    26\t    /** A stored credential. The hash is kept only for the life of one verification. */\n    27\t    public record Credential(long userId, String passwordHash, boolean active) {\n    28\t    }\n    29\t\n    30\t    /**\n    31\t     * Locks and returns the credential row for an email address.\n    32\t     *\n    33\t     * <p>The row is locked for the duration of the caller's transaction so that\n    34\t     * \"verify the password, then issue a session\" cannot interleave with a concurrent\n    35\t     * password change or deactivation.\n    36\t     *\n    37\t     * @param email an address already validated by {@link InputValidator}\n    38\t     */\n    39\t    public Optional<Credential> findForUpdate(Connection connection, String email) throws SQLException {\n    40\t        try (PreparedStatement ps = connection.prepareStatement(SELECT_FOR_UPDATE)) {\n    41\t            ps.setString(1, email);\n    42\t            ps.setMaxFieldSize(512); // bound what a hostile row can make us read\n    43\t            try (ResultSet rs = ps.executeQuery()) {\n    44\t                if (!rs.next()) {\n    45\t                    return Optional.empty();\n    46\t                }\n    47\t                long userId = rs.getLong(\"user_id\");\n    48\t                String hash = rs.getString(\"password_hash\");\n    49\t                boolean active = rs.getBoolean(\"is_active\");\n    50\t                if (rs.wasNull() || userId <= 0 || hash == null || hash.isBlank()) {\n    51\t                    // Malformed row: treat as unusable rather than as a pass.\n    52\t                    return Optional.of(new Credential(Math.max(userId, 1), \"\", false));\n    53\t                }\n    54\t                return Optional.of(new Credential(userId, hash, active));\n    55\t            }\n    56\t        }\n    57\t    }\n    58\t\n    59\t    /** Creates or replaces a user's password hash. Used by the admin tool only. */\n    60\t    public void saveCredential(Connection connection, String email, String encodedHash)\n    61\t            throws SQLException {\n    62\t        try (PreparedStatement update = connection.prepareStatement(UPSERT)) {\n    63\t            update.setString(1, encodedHash);\n    64\t            update.setString(2, email);\n    65\t            if (update.executeUpdate() > 0) {\n    66\t                return;\n    67\t            }\n    68\t        }\n    69\t        try (PreparedStatement insert = connection.prepareStatement(INSERT)) {\n    70\t            insert.setString(1, email);\n    71\t            insert.setString(2, encodedHash);\n    72\t            insert.executeUpdate();\n    73\t        }\n    74\t    }\n    75\t}\n===== Database.java =====\n     1\tpackage com.example.auth;\n     2\t\n     3\timport java.sql.Connection;\n     4\timport java.sql.DriverManager;\n     5\timport java.sql.SQLException;\n     6\timport java.util.Properties;\n     7\t\n     8\t/**\n     9\t * Hands out JDBC connections. Credentials come from {@link AuthConfig} and are passed\n    10\t * through driver properties, never embedded in the URL (which tends to end up in logs).\n    11\t */\n    12\tpublic final class Database {\n    13\t\n    14\t    private final AuthConfig config;\n    15\t\n    16\t    public Database(AuthConfig config) {\n    17\t        this.config = config;\n    18\t    }\n    19\t\n    20\t    /** Opens a connection with auto-commit off, so callers control transactions. */\n    21\t    public Connection openTransaction() throws SQLException {\n    22\t        Properties props = new Properties();\n    23\t        if (!config.dbUser().isEmpty()) {\n    24\t            props.setProperty(\"user\", config.dbUser());\n    25\t        }\n    26\t        char[] password = config.dbPassword();\n    27\t        if (password.length > 0) {\n    28\t            props.setProperty(\"password\", new String(password));\n    29\t        }\n    30\t        Connection connection = DriverManager.getConnection(config.jdbcUrl(), props);\n    31\t        props.clear();\n    32\t        try {\n    33\t            connection.setAutoCommit(false);\n    34\t
